NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 18 Version date: April 2, 2018 2 Therapeutic Context Analysis of Condition Cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma (CSCC) is the second most common human cancer in the United States, with an estimated annual incidence of 700,000 cases (Karia et al. 2013). While most cases are localized tumors amenable to curative resection, approximately 8% of patients will experience a local recurrence, 5% of patients will develop nodal metastases, and an estimated 2% of patients will die from their disease (Thompson et al. 2016, Kauver et. al. 2015). In addition to being a life‐threatening disease, CSCC causes significant functional morbidities and cosmetic deformities based on tumors commonly arising in the head and neck region and invading blood vessels, nerves and vital organs such as the eye or ear (Kauver et al. 2015). Precise incidences of overall disease, high‐risk disease , and survival outcomes are not available because these cancers are grouped with other nonmelanoma skin cancers (NMSC) in the Surveillance, Epidemiology, and End Results (SEER) database. High‐risk patients are those who have recurrent disease or who are at high risk for recurrence, lymph node metastases , or distant metastases. Patients who have recurrence of disease after having a primary tumor resection will develop local or distant metastases 25% of the time (Kauver et al. 2015). Factors associated with recurrence and poor prognosis in CSCC include tumor size > 2 cm, tumor depth > 2 mm (Breslow thickness), perineural invasion, host immunosuppression, poorly differentiated histology and location on the ear, temple , or lip (Thompson et al. 2016, Kauver et al. 2015). Ten‐year survival rates are less than 20% for patients with regional lymph‐node involvement. For those patients who develop distant metastases, the median survival time is estimated to be less than two years (Stratigos et al. 2015). Analysis of Current Treatment Options The standard of care for locoregional CSCC is complete surgical resection with a minimum margin of 5 mm for low risk tumors and 10 mm for high risk tumors. If there is nodal involvement, a regional lymph node dissection is recommended, and adjuvant radiation therapy is utilized in most cases (Stratigos et al. 2015; NCCN Guidelines, VI.2017). Treatment options are limited for the small subset of patients with CSCC who develop local recurrences, unresectable disease, or distant metastatic disease. The only FDA‐approved treatment for locally advanced or metastatic disease is cemiplimab‐rwlc (Libtayo®), a PD‐1 blocking antibody. Cemiplimab was approved by the FDA in July 2018 for the treatment of patients with metastatic CSCC or locally advanced CSCC who are not candidates for curative surgery or curative radiation. Approval was based on the results of two open‐label, multi‐center, non‐randomized, multicohort studies: Study 1423 (NCT023883212) and Study 1540 (NCT02760498). A total of 26 patients with CSCC were enrolled in Study 1423 and 82 patients were enrolled in Study 1540. The major efficacy outcome measures were confirmed objective response rate (ORR) as assessed by independent central review (ICR) and ICR‐assessed duration of response. For Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 120>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 19 Version date: April 2, 2018 patients with metastatic CSCC without externally visible target lesions, ORR was determined by Response Evaluation Criteria in Solid Tumors (RECIST 1.1). For patients with externally visible target lesions (locally advanced or metastatic CSCC), ORR was determined by a composite endpoint that integrated ICR assessments of radiologic data (RECIST 1.1) and digital medical photography (WHO criteria). In total, 108 patients were evaluated, 75 with metastatic disease and 33 with locally advanced disease. Confirmed ORR was 46.7% (95% CI: 35.1%, 58.6%) in the metastatic cohort and 48.5% (95% CI: 30.8%, 66.5%) in the locally advanced cohort with a combined ORR of 47.2% (95% CI: 37.5%, 57.1%). Duration of response (DOR) ranged from 1 month to greater than 15.2 months with 60% (21/35) of responders maintaining responses for 6 months of longer. Other treatments for advanced CSCC are off‐label use of platinum‐based chemotherapy and cetuximab. There is limited data on the utility of such regiments. Reference ID: 4627964

NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 20 Version date: April 2, 2018 3 Regulatory Background U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History Pembrolizumab, under the proprietary name Keytruda, was first approved for use in the United States in 2014 for the treatment of patients with unresectable or metastatic melanoma and disease progression following ipilimumab and, if BRAF V600 mutation positive, a BRAF inhibitor. As of 30‐Sep‐2019, Keytruda® has received traditional approval in the US for the following indications:  Treatment of patients with unresectable or metastatic melanoma.  Adjuvant treatment of patients with melanoma with involvement of lymph node(s) following complete resection.  In combination with pemetrexed and platinum chemotherapy, as first‐line treatment of patients with metastatic nonsquamous NSCLC, with no EGFR or ALK genomic tumor aberrations.  In combination with carboplatin and either paclitaxel or paclitaxel protein‐bound, as first‐ line treatment of patients with metastatic squamous NSCLC.  First‐line treatment of patients with NSCLC expressing PD‐L1 [Tumor Proportion Score (TPS) ≥1%)] as determined by an FDA‐approved test, with no EGFR or ALK genomic tumor aberrations, and is: – Stage III where patients are not candidates for surgical resection or definitive chemoradiation, or – metastatic.  Treatment of patients with metastatic NSCLC whose tumors express PD‐L1 (TPS ≥1%) as determined by an FDA‐approved test, with disease progression on or after platinum‐ containing chemotherapy. Patients with EGFR or ALK genomic tumor aberrations should have disease progression on FDA‐approved therapy for these aberrations prior to receiving KEYTRUDA.  In combination with platinum and FU for the first‐line treatment of patients with metastatic or with unresectable, recurrent HNSCC.  First‐line treatment of patients with metastatic or with unresectable, recurrent HNSCC whose tumors express PD‐L1 [Combined Positive Score (CPS) ≥1] as determined by an FDA‐approved test.  Treatment of patients with recurrent or metastatic HNSCC with disease progression on or after platinum‐containing chemotherapy.  In combination with axitinib, for the first‐line treatment of patients with advanced RCC. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity  May 5, 2017 : Merck submitted a new IND containing KEYNOTE‐629, "A Phase 2, Open‐Label, Single Arm Study to Evaluate the Safety and Efficacy of Pembrolizumab in Participants with Recurrent or Metastatic Cutaneous Squamous Cell Carcinoma (R/M cSCC).” Study was allowed to proceed June 4, 2017  December 6, 2017: FDA issued an information request related to KEYNOTE‐629 requesting an estimate of the expected effect size of pembrolizumab for the patients enrolled in the study and justification of the sample size based on the lower bound of the 95% confidence interval (CI) of this response rate. On January 5, 2018, Merck provided a response.  July 20, 2018: Type C meeting held to obtain feedback from FDA on the clinical development program of pembrolizumab in CSCC, specifically the ongoing study KEYNOTE‐ 620 and the proposed Study KEYNOTE‐630. Key points include: o FDA agreed the proposed population of patients with incurably recurrence and metastatic CSCC in the KEYNOTE‐629 protocol represented a patient population with an unmet medical need and demonstration of a durable ORR of clinically meaningful magnitude with an acceptable safety profile could support a supplemental BLA filing for pembrolizumab for the treatment of patients with metastatic CSCC. o FDA agreed the proposed population of patients with unresectable locally advanced (LA) CSCC has an unmet medical need; however, advised Merck to evaluate patients with metastatic disease independently from patients with LA CSCC as the two conditions have different prognoses and a different treatment effect may be observed in the two populations. FDA also noted that it will be essential to have an adequate sample size in both populations to demonstrate clinical benefit. FDA acknowledged the proposed population is a rare patient population. FDA also stated that based on results, results in both cohorts in labeling might be able to be pooled. o FDA agreed that imaging and photography information could provide support for the assessment of lesions in the high‐risk unresectable LA CSCC population. o FDA stated the proposed statistical analysis plan (SAP) for KEYNOTE‐629 appeared to be acceptable and reiterated that it was essential to have an adequate sample size in both populations to demonstrate clinical benefit. o FDA stated that whether the data from the single arm KEYNOTE‐629 trial will support regular approval will depend on the results observed. Merck will need to provide justification for the proposed effect size and duration of effect to be considered evidence of direct benefit. The patient population treated and the safety profile of the drug will also be considered for regulatory decision making. o FDA noted the overall study designed appeared acceptable. FDA recommended conducting the interim analysis for overall survival (OS) at the time of final analysis Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 124>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 23 Version date: April 2, 2018 for event free survival. During the meeting, Merck confirmed they will use O’Brian‐ Flemming rules to allocate alpha for the interim analysis. FDA stated the approach was acceptable. o FDA did not object to the proposed primary endpoint of EFS and secondary endpoint of OS. o The meeting included several questions regarding PROs  FDA noted that the PRO instrument EORTC‐QLQ‐C30 maybe a reasonable choice to assess some function impacts, the agency recommended that Merck focus the PRO measurement strategy on assessment of core disease and treatment symptoms and the associated impacts.  FDA stated that the PRO instrument EQ‐5D‐5L lacks evidence of content validity for use in estimating clinical benefit for labeling claims.  With respect to PROs, FDA noted that patients’ knowledge of treatment assignments (and single arm design) may lead to systemic overestimation of the treatment effect, the magnitude of which is currently unknown.  With respect to Skindex‐16, FDA noted that while this generic instrument, which is not specific to CSCC, appeared to include some concepts that may be relevant for this drug development program, there may be important concepts that are missing.   Merck indicated that the PROS will be administered in the following order: EQ‐5D‐5L, EORTC QLQ‐C30, Skindex‐16. FDA recommended that Merck order assessments based on the PRO endpoint positioning to minimize missing data in the target instrument.  FDA instructed Merck to conduct analyses for the EORTC instrument using both raw and transformed score to help facilitate data interpretation.  FDA did not agree that a 10‐point threshold in the EOTC‐0QLC‐C30 is a clinically meaningful change. The threshold for meaningful change should be derived from anchor‐based methods supplemented with empirical cumulative distribution function and provability density function curves.  FDA recommended that Merck include anchor scales in the randomized clinical trial to help interpret meaningful score changes in the target PRO instruments.  FDA stated that PRO assessments will need to be culturally adapted and adequately translated for all intended study populations for use in multinational trials.  FDA advised that concomitant medications that may affect the interpretation of the concepts being measured such as pain or itch be carefully recorded. o FDA directed Merck to provide analysis of confirmed ORR based on subgroups defined by extent of prior therapy. NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 29 Version date: April 2, 2018 6 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy KEYNOTE ‐ 629 Trial Design KEYNOTE‐629 is a phase 2, multicenter, open‐label, single‐arm study to evaluate the safety and efficacy of pembrolizumab in patients with R/M CSCC or LA unresectable CSCC. The study was ongoing at the time of this submission. Figure 1 shows the schema of the study design for KEYNOTE‐629. The patients included in the efficacy analyses for this review are those enrolled in the R/M CSCC cohort and who received at least one dose of pembrolizumab. Figure 1 KEYNOTE‐629 Study Schema a D8/D15 safety visits were required for participants in the R/M cSCC cohort only. b Cycles were to repeat if treatment was administered beyond Cycle 6 c Disease progression or toxicity or 35 infusions (24 months of treatment) Note: If participants achieved CR, PR or SD and stopped pembrolizumab, and subsequently experienced disease progression, they may have been eligible for an additional year (17 cycles) of pembrolizumab treatment. Enrollment criteria applicable to the R/M cohort include:  Age ≥ 18 years  Histologically confirmed CSCC as the primary site of malignancy.  Metastatic disease, defined as disseminated disease distant to the initial/primary site of diagnosis, and/or locally recurrent disease that has not been previously treated (with either surgery or radiotherapy) and is not curable by either surgery and/or radiotherapy. Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 131>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 30 Version date: April 2, 2018  Measurable disease based on RECIST 1.1 as assessed by central review. Lesions situated in a previously irradiated area are considered measurable if progression has been demonstrated in such lesions.  ECOG performance status of 0 to 1.  Patients may not have received prior therapy with an anti‐PD‐1, anti‐PD‐L1, or anti‐PD‐ L2 agent or with an agent directed to other stimulatory or co‐inhibitory T‐cell receptor (e.g., CTLA‐4, OX‐40, CD137). Patients received KEYTRUDA 200 mg intravenously every 3 weeks until documented disease progression, unacceptable toxicity, or a maximum of 24 months. Patients with initial radiographic disease progression could continue to receive additional doses of KEYTRUDA during confirmation of progression unless disease progression was symptomatic, rapidly progressive, required urgent intervention, or occurred with a decline in performance status. Assessment of tumor status was performed every 6 weeks during the first year and every 9 weeks during the second year. Study Endpoints Primary Endpoint: In KEYNOTE‐629, the primary efficacy endpoint was overall response rate (ORR) as assessed by blinded independent central review committee (BICR) per RECIST 1.1. ORR was defined as the proportion of patients with complete or partial response based on RECIST 1.1. The initial documented response was confirmed after at least 4 weeks. Secondary endpoints:  Duration of response (DOR): DOR was defined as the time from first documented evidence of complete response (CR) or partial response (PR) until disease progression or death due to any cause, whichever occurs first.  Disease control rate (DCR): DCR was defined as CR, PR, or stable disease (SD) for at least 12 weeks.  Progression‐free survival (PFS) per RECIST 1.1 by BICR: PFS was defined as the time from first day of study treatment to the first documented disease progression according to RECIST 1.1 or death due to any cause, whichever occurs first. If a patient did not have a documented date of progression or death, PFS was censored at the date of the last adequate assessment.  Overall survival (OS): OS was defined as the time from first day of study treatment to death due to any cause. Censoring was performed using the date of last known contact for those who are alive at the time of analysis. Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 132>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 31 Version date: April 2, 2018 Statistical Analysis Plan Sample Size Approximately 100 patients with R/M CSCC and 50 patients with LA unresectable patients were planned to be enrolled in this trial. With 100 patients in R/M CSCC cohort and with an expected ORR of at least 30%, the study has >95% power to have the lower bound of the 95% CI >15%. Analysis datasets All the patients who had received at least one dose of study treatment were included in the efficacy and safety analysis. This analysis population was also referred as the “All Participants as Treated (APaT).” Efficacy Analyses For the primary endpoint, ORR per RECIST 1.1 by ICR, point estimates of the proportion of patients with confirmed CR or PR in the APaT population and the corresponding two‐sided 95% Clopper‐Pearson exact confidence interval were reported. Similar methods were used to analyze DCR. For DOR, PFS, and OS, the median Kaplan‐Meier (KM) estimates were provided. For OS analysis, patients alive at the time of analysis were censored at the date of the last contact. For DOR, patients who were alive, had not progressed, had not initiated new anti‐ cancer treatment, had not been lost to follow‐up, and had a disease assessment within approximately 5 months of the data cutoff date were considered to be ongoing responders at the time of analysis. For PFS, patients who had not experienced progression or death were censored at the date of the last adequate assessment. Interim Analysis and Multiplicity Periodic data monitoring for safety will be performed by the sponsor. No multiplicity adjustment was required. Protocol Violations/Deviations Important protocol deviations were pre‐defined as any protocol deviation that may significantly impact the quality or integrity of key study data or that may significantly affect a patient’s safety, well‐being, or rights. Clinically important deviations are defined as those that may compromise critical data analysis pertaining to primary efficacy and/or safety endpoints or the participant’s safety. A total of 14 important protocol deviations were reported for 13 (12.4%) patients in the R/M cohort. Only 1 deviation, tumor debulking in a target lesion without consultation, was classified as a clinically important deviation. The remaining deviations involved violations in discontinuation criteria (1), informed consent (3), safety reporting (6), study intervention (1), and trial procedures (2). FDA Reviewer Comment: It is unlikely that the protocol deviations have an impact on the study results. Efficacy Results – Secondary or exploratory COA (PRO) endpoints Patient reported outcomes (PROs) in this study were exploratory endpoints and were evaluated using EORTC QLQ‐C30 and the EQ‐5D‐5L questionnaires. PRO questionnaires were completed by the patients prior to dosing at Cycles 1, 2 and 3, every 2 cycles (i.e., every 6 weeks) through Year 1, every 3 cycles (i.e., every 9 weeks) from Year 1 until End of Treatment, and at the 30‐ day Safety Follow‐up Visit. Analysis of PRO assessments included evaluation of change from baseline in EORTC QLQ‐C30 global health status/QoL, functioning and symptom scores, and EQ‐ 5D VAS score. PRO analyses were based on the PRO PPT population, defined as all enrolled participants who received at least one dose of study treatment and had baseline and at least 1 post‐baseline PRO assessments. The sample size of the PRO PPT population for the EORTC QLQ‐ C30 was 99 and for EQ‐5D was 100. Table 8 shows the compliance rates for both questionnaires at each collection time point, where compliance is defined as the proportion of subjects who completed the PRO questionnaire among those who are expected to complete the questionnaire at this time point. NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 42 Version date: April 2, 2018 Integrated Review of Effectiveness This supplemental BLA was supported by a single trial, KEYNOTE‐629. No pooled analysis was performed. Integrated Assessment of Effectiveness The efficacy of pembrolizumab to treat patients with CSCC was based on a single multicenter, non‐randomized, open‐label trial, KEYNOTE‐629. This supplemental application only included data for the 105 patients enrolled to the recurrent or metastatic CSCC cohort. The major efficacy measures were ORR and DOR per RECIST v1.1 as assessed by BICR. The ORR in the 105 patients was 34% (95% CI: 25.3, 44.2). Among the 36 responding patients, the DOR ranged from 2.7 ‐ 13.1+ months with 25 patients (69%) having responses of 6 months or longer and 14 patients (39%) having responses of 9 months or longer. The highly durable response rate of pembrolizumab demonstrated in KEYNOTE‐629 represents substantial evidence of effectiveness. Reference ID: 4627964

NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 43 Version date: April 2, 2018 Review of the Safety Safety Review Approach The primary study used to evaluate safety for this indication‐specific population was data from 105 patients enrolled to the R/M cohort from KEYNOTE‐629, all who received at least one dose of pembrolizumab 200 mg once every 3 weeks. Data from a pooled population (N=2799), referred to as the referenced safety dataset (RSD) and consisting of 1567 patients with advanced melanoma (from studies KEYNOTE‐001, KEYNOTE‐002, and KEYNOTE‐006) and 1232 patients with non‐small cell lung cancer (from KEYNOTE‐001 and KEYNOTE‐010) who received pembrolizumab monotherapy, was evaluated in support of this application. Review of the Safety Database Overall Exposure All patients in the R/M cohort received at least 1 dose of pembrolizumab 200 mg once every 3 weeks. Table 9 summarizes the duration of treatment and number of infusions for the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 and the pooled population. The duration and number of infusions is comparable between the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 and the RSD. NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 44 Version date: April 2, 2018 Relevant characteristics of the safety population The demographics and baseline disease characteristics for the safety population, which is identical to the APaT, are shown in Table 4 and Table 5. Adequacy of the safety database: The overall safety database for the patient population for which a new indication is requested in this supplemental BLA application is small; however, there is extensive experience with pembrolizumab at the dosage proposed in this supplement. Therefore, a large safety population in this specific indication is not required. The exposure of patients with R/M CSSC to pembrolizumab is comparable to the RSD. Overall, the safety database is adequate to assess the toxicity of pembrolizumab in this rare patient population. Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality FDA performed the following analyses to assess the quality and consistency of the safety data: 1) Comparison of coding: This reviewer compared the coding of the actual reported term (AETERM) to the lowest level term coded in the MedDRA hierarchy (AELLT) for the 1019 adverse events that were reported for the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629. Overall the coding was of high quality and consistent. 2) Comparison of treatment discontinuations due to adverse events as reported in the subject level ADSL and as reported in ADAE variable AECN (action taken) found 17 patients who had an AE where the action taken was “drug withdrawn” while the ADSL reported 13 patients discontinued due to adverse event. 3) FDA confirmed the key safety results reported by the Applicant in the Summary of Clinical Safety, the Clinical Study Report, and all data added to the label with this supplement for the R/M Cohort of KEYNOTE‐629. Analyses using the RSD have been verified as part of previous supplemental applications and were not independently verified for this supplement. Categorization of Adverse Events The KEYNOTE‐629 protocol was designed to record all adverse events regardless of severity. Reporting was required for all AEs occurring within 30 days and all SAEs occurring within 90 days after the last dose of pembrolizumab. The AEs were graded 1 to 5 according to the NCI Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) Version 4.0. All AEs were coded using the Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) version 20.1. Reference ID: 4627964

Safety Results Deaths Twelve patients (11%) in the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 had a fatal TEAE, not including 4 patients with a fatal event of progressive disease. Table 10 summarizes the narratives for the fatal events for these 12 patients. The frequency of deaths in this cohort is higher than observed in the RSD (3.9%). Reviewer Comment: The Applicant did not provide its own assessment of causality. Only for 1 fatal event, cranial nerve disorder, did the investigator assess the event as related to study drug. This reviewer concurs with the investigator’s assessment. The Investigator assessed the remaining 11 fatal events as unrelated to pembrolizumab. In 7 cases, this reviewer agrees; however, for 3 events ‐‐ respiratory failure, acute respiratory failure, and cardiac failure, the reviewer found insufficiency information to rule out pembrolizumab as a contributing factor, given its established toxicity profile. In addition, for the fatal case of C. difficile infection in a patient who initially developed diarrhea at Day 30, in the absence of a biopsy ruling out an immune mediated component, this reviewer assesses pembrolizumab as at least possibly contributing to the event. The reviewer’s assessment of causality does not identify any new safety signal. Reference ID: 4627964

Serious Adverse Events Serious adverse events (SAEs) are defined as an AE that result in death, is life‐threatening, requires inpatient hospitalization or prolongation of existing hospitalization, results in persistent or significant disability/incapacity, or is congenital anomaly/birth defect in the offspring of a participant taking the product, regardless of time to diagnosis. In the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629, a total of 56 serious adverse events (SAEs), not including fatal events, were reported in 37 (35%) patients. The only preferred terms (PT) reported in > 1 patient were: anemia (2.9%), malignant neoplasm progression (2.9%), infected neoplasm (1.9%), myocardial infarction (1.9%), pneumonia (1.9%), and transaminases increased (1.9%). At the higher level group term (HLGT), the following terms were reported in > 1 patient: infections ‐pathogen unspecified (9%), miscellaneous and site unspecified neoplasms malignant and unspecified ( 3.8%), neoplasm related morbidities (3.8%), anemias nonhemolytic an marrow depression (2.9%), gastrointestinal signs and symptoms (2.9%), coronary artery disorders (1.9%), hepatobiliary investigations (1.9%), musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders NEC (1.9%), and renal disorders (excl nephropathies (1.9%). These findings are generally consistent with the frequencies observed in the RDS and where increased, for example infected neoplasm, reflect morbidities associated with the underlying disease. Assessment of SAEs does not identify a new or more concerning safety signal. Discontinuations and/or Interruptions Due to Adverse Effects The KEYNOTE‐629 protocol specified dose modification and toxicity management guidelines consistent with the pembrolizumab label. The protocol did not permit dose reductions. In the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629, 13 patients (12%) discontinued treatment due to an adverse event, not including the event of malignant neoplasm progression. No PT occurred more than once. When considering only worsening to Grade 3‐4, there is no laboratory parameter for which the incidence is ≥ 5% higher in the R/M cohort. Overall, analysis of laboratory data for the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 did not identify a new serious safety signal. NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 58 Version date: April 2, 2018 Analysis of Submission ‐ Specific Safety Issues Keytruda is currently approved for numerous indications (see Section 3.1) and has a well‐ established safety profile. The current labeling identifies the following adverse reactions: infusion related reactions and immune mediated adverse reactions. The warnings and precautions section of labeling specifically identifies the following immune mediated adverse reactions: pneumonitis, colitis, hepatitis, endocrinopathies, and skin reactions. KEYNOTE‐629 assessed the following immune‐mediated adverse reactions (IMARs) as adverse events of special interest (AEOSIs): Pneumonitis, Colitis, Hepatitis, Nephritis, Adrenal Insufficiency, Hypophysitis, Hyperthyroidism, Hypothyroidism, Thyroiditis, Type 1 Diabetes Mellitus, Skin‐B (Exfoliative and Bullous Dermatitis), Skin‐A (Non‐exfoliative/bullous rash), Uveitis, Pancreatitis, Myositis, Guillain‐Barre Syndrome, Myocarditis, Encephalitis, Sarcoidosis, Myasthenic Syndrome, Infusion reaction. In addition, infusion related reactions (IRR) were considered an AEOSI. 15.3 15.3 lists the set of preferred terms used to identify each AEOSI. In the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629, 22% of patients (23/105) had at least one immune mediated AEOSI, with 6% (6/105) having at least one Grade 3 immune‐mediated AEOSI. This is comparable to the RDS where 21% of patients had at least one AEOSI (6% Grade 3‐5). In the R/M Cohort, no AEOSI > Grade 3 was recorded. Table 15 summarizes the frequency of specific IMARs occurring in at least 1 patient in the R/M Cohort. Analysis of the IMARs in the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 did not identify any new safety signal in patients receiving pembrolizumab for CSCC. NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 59 Version date: April 2, 2018 One patient (1%) had an infusion related reaction, which was Grade 3. The frequency of infusion related reaction in the RSD is 2.5%. Reviewer Comment: The fatal case of cranial neuropathy was not identified as an IMAR although the investigator and this reviewer assessed the event as likely related to pembrolizumab. Based on the mechanism of action of pembrolizumab and the known risk of immune mediated neuropathy, this case possibly represents a fatal IMAR. The Applicant did not flag any TEAEs with the AEBODSYS (SOC) “Nervous system Disorder” as an AEOSI. This reviewer retrieved all TEAEs in ADAE with AEBODSYS “Nervous System Disorder” and identified the following TEAEs that possibly represent immune‐mediated neuropathies: peripheral sensory neuropathy (x2), neuralgia (x2). The omission of these TEAEs as possible IMARs does not alter the overall risk: benefit assessment. Clinical Outcome Assessment (COA) Analyses Informing Safety/Tolerability Not applicable. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups Gender Table 16 summarizes TEAEs for patients enrolled to the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 by gender. In the R/M cohort, 76% of patients were male and 24% were female. Overall, males had a higher incidence (≥ 10% difference) of SAEs, Grade 3‐4 TEAEs, and TEAEs leading to interruption. There are no individual PTs for which the difference in frequency is ≥ 10% between male and female patients. Some differences are to be expected due to change alone, and given the limited number of patients in each group, a true interaction between gender and these differences cannot be concluded. Thus, no gender specific labeling is indicated. Reference ID: 4627964

NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 61 Version date: April 2, 2018 Ethnicity Race and ethnicity data is not complete for a significant percentage of the patents enrolled on the R/M cohort and therefore no subgroup analysis for this parameter was performed. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials None Additional Safety Explorations Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development The Applicant did not conduct carcinogenicity studies. Human Reproduction and Pregnancy Fertility studies have not been conducted with pembrolizumab. Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth KEYNOTE‐629 did not include pediatric patients. Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound No studies have been conducted to evaluate the abuse potential with pembrolizumab. The potential for drug abuse or dependence is expected to be negligible based on the mechanism of action. There have been no reports of drug abuse with pembrolizumab. Safety in the Postmarket Setting Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience The safety profile of pembrolizumab is well established and summarized in the most recently submitted Periodic Safety Update Report covering the period 04‐Seo‐2018 through 03‐Mar‐ 2019. Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting Although the CSCC‐specific safety set is small, analysis for this supplemental application did not identify any potentially new or more severe safety signal. There is no biological reason to expect patient receiving pembrolizumab for CSCC will have a different safety profile. No difference in pembrolizumab administration in the postmarket setting relative to KEYNOTE‐629 is expected. Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 163>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 62 Version date: April 2, 2018 Integrated Assessment of Safety The evaluation of pembrolizumab 200 mg IV once every 3 weeks in patients with recurrent or metastatic CSCC was based on 105 patients enrolled in the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629, all who received at least one dose of study drug. Analysis of the safety data with the application did not identify any serious safety signal not already included in the current labeling. Overall, pembrolizumab was well tolerated in patients with CSCC. The median duration of treatment was 5.8 months (range 0.03 to 16.13 months) with 49% of patients receiving study drug for 6 months or more. Table 18 summarizes the major safety results for the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629. While almost all patients had at least 1 TEAE, an AE led to discontinuation in only 12% of patients and treatment interruption in 27%. The majority of events were Grade 1 or 2. Twelve patients (11%) had a fatal adverse event, however, for at least half of the cases, pembrolizumab was unlike to be related to the fatality. Only one fatal event, cranial nerve disorder, was likely related to pembrolizumab. For the remaining 5 cases, there is insufficient evidence to rule out pembrolizumab as a contributing causative agent based on its known safety profile; however, in all cases, there were confounding comorbidities. The frequency of immune‐mediated AEOSIs was 22%. Most of the immune‐mediated AEOSIs were endocrinopathies. The majority of all AEOSIs were Grade 1‐2. Table 18 Major Safety Findings R/M Cohort KEYNOTE‐629 Any TEAE 103 (98) Fatal Adverse Event* 12 (11) SAE (not including fatal events) 37 (35) Grade 3‐4 44 (42) TEAE (Grade 1‐4) 102 (97) Treatment Discontinuations due to an AE* 13 (12) Treatment Interruption due to an AE 28 (27) Immune Mediated AEOSI 23 (22) Source: Reviewer table generated from KEYNOTE‐629 R/M Cohort: ADAE *Not including the event “malignant disease progression”. The AE profile based on the types and frequency of most common AEs and the immune‐ mediated adverse reactions is similar between the RSD and the R/M cohort. Overall, the safety profile for pembrolizumab in the KEYNOTE‐629 R/M CSCC cohort safety dataset is consistent with the established safety profile of pembrolizumab monotherapy. Statistical Issues No major statistical issues were encountered during this review. A limitation for the review of this data is that the time‐to‐event endpoints such as PFS and OS could not be adequately characterized due to the single‐arm nature of the study. Reference ID: 4627964

<<<PAGE 164>>>
NDA/BLA Multi‐disciplinary Review and Evaluation {Insert Application Type and Number} {Insert Product Trade and Generic Name} 63 Version date: April 2, 2018 Conclusions and Recommendations The submitted data has provided substantial evidence of the safety and effectiveness of pembrolizumab for the treatment of patients with recurrent or metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma. The totality of evidence based on the results of the R/M cohort from KEYNOTE‐ 629 demonstrated that pembrolizumab provided a clinically meaningful benefit as evidenced by a high rate of durable responses in the patients. The safety profile of pembrolizumab in the R/M cohort of KEYNOTE‐629 is consistent with the established safety profile of pembrolizumab, and no new safety risks were identified. The review team therefore recommends regular approval of pembrolizumab for the treatment of patients with recurrent or metastatic cutaneous squamous cell carcinoma. Reference ID: 4627964
